NFL predictions: Our picks for Giants-Commanders, the full Week 10 slate

📅 Nov 12, 2026  |  Category: Business  |  Read Source →

The New York Giants and Washington Commanders kick off Week 10 of the NFL season Thursday night at what promises to be a rainy, chilly MetLife Stadium. Can the Giants solidify their NFC East lead, or will the race for the division title tighten? Here is how the Big Blue View staff sees that game, and the rest of the Week 10 matchups.

Which version of the Giants will show up on Thursday night?

Will we get the sloppy Giants that came out flat against the Philadelphia Eagles, or the tough, physical, and technically sound Giants that showed up against the Houston Texans? John Harbaugh's 9-1 record home record on Thursday Nights is encouraging.

I would say that this is a game the Giants should win on paper, particularly with Washington missing some of their best players and their starting center in the concussion protocol. However, I'm also not convinced that these Giants can play a normal game and just take care of business. I keep waiting for the Giants to play a complete game and execute on offense and defense, but this team seems unable to be both consistent and explosive.

I hope the offensive line comes prepared to bully an injured Washington defensive line and J.J. McCarthy isn't forced into low percentage throws every third down by long yardage situations. Likewise, I hope that Dennard Wilson comes out swinging on defense, but also has a counterpunch ready in case Jayden Daniels has an answer to the Cover-0 blitz.

I'm picking the Giants here, but they can't afford to take Washington lightly or give Jayden Daniels the chance to catch his breath and swing the game.

This is the Giants' second consecutive must-win game. You wouldn't think that when 5-2-1 plays 2-5-1, but (1) the Giants haven't beaten the Commanders since Jayden Daniels became their quarterback (even though Daniels didn't play in one of those losses), (2) despite their record, Washington has beaten the Seahawks and only narrowly lost to the 49ers and Rams while the Giants were beaten soundly by the Rams, the only good team they've faced, and still have the 49ers and Seahawks coming up, along with the Jaguars and Lions. A division loss greatly weakens their hold on the NFC East lead, their only real path to the playoffs.

Terry McLaurin is out for the season, but in his place is Stefon Diggs, who did not face the Giants in the first game. Jacory Crosky-Merritt, who beat the Giants' run D for a 33-yard TD right off the bat in the first game, is also out. The Commanders are further decimated by injuries since their last meeting to key starters such as DT Daron Payne and IOL Lucas Patrick. So really the Giants should prevail. The problem is…containing the running of Daniels, which they did not do well in their first meeting (103 yards, by far his highest of the season, and his only 2 rushing TDs of the season). Hopefully Dennard Wilson will have a plan for that. If so, and if the Giants execute it, then the Giants can win.

The rematch of the NFL's only tie game of the season will be played in prime time on Thursday night, and for the ninth time in nine games, the Giants are playing a team that doesn't have a winning record. But you can throw the records out the window: The 5-2-1 Giants, at home, are just 1.5 point favorites over the 2-5-1 Commanders. Go figure…

Big Blue won with defense and a strong kicking game on Sunday in Philadelphia, with Arvel Reese perhaps becoming a star in real time. It was an ugly win, with the offense producing just 19 points. The Giants will need more than that against Jayden Daniels and company on Thursday night. Cam Skattebo is expected back, and that's good news. New York couldn't run the ball on Sunday, which stagnated their offense. Right tackle Jermaine Eluemunor (calf) might miss this game, however, and the O-line struggled at Philadelphia as it was.

J.J. McCarthy wasn't terrible on Sunday, but he wasn't especially good either and now he's dealing with a hip pointer. Jameis Winston led New York on its only touchdown drive whileMcCarthy was temporarily knocked from the game. McCarthy hasn't thrown a TD pass (or an interception) in either of his two starts.

This figures to be a close game, just like the first one. It'll be fun to watch standout rookies Reese and Sonny Styles — college teammates at Ohio State last season — leading the way for each defense. Seriously – how did anyone score on the Buckeyes? Anyway, Dennard Wilson needs to find a way to keep Daniels from gashing his defense with his legs. He ran for 103 yards and two scores in the first game.

This game could come down to intangibles like turnovers and special teams. The G-Men have been winning (or tying) close games and that's a reflection on good coaching to a degree, and favorable luck. In a game that should be close, who am I to pick against that?

We are not picking games by the spread here, but I am surprised the Giants are just 1.5-point favorites. That indicates a continuing lack of real belief in the Giants in the betting market. The Giants understand the opportunity they have in front of them, and how important this game is.

In the rainy, windy, miserable conditions at MetLife Stadium Thursday evening I think you have to expect what has become a typical Giants game this season — close, somewhat sloppy, decided by who makes the plays and who properly handles the situations at the end of the game.

I have far more faith in the Giants to do that than I do the Commanders.

We all remember the Commanders stunning clock mismanagement at the end of the Week 5 tie with the Giants. That sort of questionable decision-making has continued to plague Washington, which is 1-3-1 in games decided by one score.

If you could the Arizona game, which I do since it was decided on the game's final play, seven of the Giants eight games this season were one-score games. The Giants are 5-1-1 in those games. They have lived on the edge, and have shown an ability to survive.

Is that formula sustainable forever? Maybe not. It does, though, make me feel good about the Giants' chances on Thursday.

What to know about Falcons – Chiefs in Week 10

📅 Nov 12, 2026  |  Category: Sports  |  Read Source →

The Atlanta Falcons are about to get their bye week, but they have to go through the Kansas City Chiefs first. The difference between rolling into the bye at 3-7 with the Vikings and Lions looming after it and being a 4-6 in the same boat is a potentially significant one, given that the NFC South is extremely weak and Atlanta's final five games are against the Browns, Commanders, and their division rivals.

But winning this game is going to be difficult, and more difficult for trading away Jessie Bates. Can our beloved Falcons stop being so thoroughly mediocre and triumph over an AFC juggernaut? For better or worse, we'll find out.

Here's what you need to know about the Week 10 matchup.

The Falcons, by the eye test, the more advanced statistics, and the high level ones I share every week here, are a 3-6 football team that looks like a 3-6 football team. By all those same measures, the Chiefs are one of the ten best teams in football. If this isn't an ass kicking—and especially if the Falcons actually win—it will be a real triumph.

This team is a diminished version of the Chiefs that briefly ruled the NFL. That's not to say that Kansas City is not still a contender—they are—or that they aren't still one of the best teams in the AFC. It is to say that they're no longer a juggernaut.

They did re-imagine the offense by signing Kenneth Walker to a massive free agency deal, and Walker's been one of the handful of great running backs in the league this year. Before this, the Chiefs had relied on a hodgepodge of lackluster backs, so the lift he's given them has helped offset a still-frustrating passing game. The team also acquired Justin Fields to back up Patrick Mahomes, and there's a non-zero chance we'll see Fields this week with Mahomes banged up. Eric Bieniemy is also back in the offensive coordinator role, and the offense has been noticeably better balanced than it has been in recent years.

Their draft has delivered plenty of needed young talent, even if the production has been mixed to this point. Mansoor Delane looks excellent but has only appeared in five games, defensive linemen R Mason Thomas and Peter Woods have real promise even if Woods has largely struggled, Jadon Canady is providing surprisingly strong coverage at cornerback early in his career, and running back Emmett Johnson and wide receiver Cyrus Allen have produced in their limited opportunities to this point. It's a good class that could pay real dividends down the line.

Finally, the Chiefs made a bit of a splash trade, grabbing wide receiver Josh Downs from the selling Colts. Downs is not a standout but has been a consistently productive player on some lousy Colts teams, and is now joining up as a clear top option for Mahomes once he gets his feet under him in Kansas City.

Generally, though, you know what this Chiefs team looks like. Andy Reid is the coach, Mahomes is the quarterback, the receivers are all varying degrees of frustrating, and stalwarts like Travis Kelce and Chris Jones are still puttering along at an impressively high level. The Chiefs are still very good because of that, but they're better health and more cohesion away from being a great team.

So much swings on whether the Falcons will see Patrick Mahomes or not. Justin Fields can kill Atlanta with his legs and avoids turnovers, but there's a massive drop-off from Mahomes to him that will impact an already inconsistent Chiefs passing attack.

That's important because of what Joe Burrow and Caleb Williams have done to the Falcons' secondary, which is now down a playmaking presence in Bates. Both quarterbacks annihilated Atlanta's coverage with sharp ball placement, good throws on the move, and effective movement in the face of pressure, and Mahomes can manage all that. The chief (sorry) difference here is that while the Bengals have Ja'Marr Chase and Tee Higgins and the Bears brought Colston Loveland, Rome Odunze, and Luther Burden to bear (sorry again), the Chiefs have a much more lackluster collection of playmakers. Their most effective option by far is Rashee Rice, who is always seemingly banged up but is lethal over the middle of the field, and Travis Kelce remains a plodding but useful receiving threat. Downs is an intriguing new addition, but how much time he'll see less than a week after being traded to Kansas City remains to be seen. If the Falcons can do a reasonable job of bottling those three up, Xavier Worthy is thoroughly mediocre and their fourth best receiving option is probably Walker, who is dangerous in the open field but not a threat downfield.

I targeted the passing game first here because of the intrigue around Mahomes vs. Fields, but the single biggest threat in this game is Walker. The Falcons were eaten alive by the Bucccaneers ground game two weeks ago, and Walker is the most dangerous back they'll face all season. He's 7th in the NFL in success rate, second behind Bijan Robinson in rushing yards by a measly eight yards, has yet to fumble once, and is averaging more yards per game than any other back in football. Atlanta's run defense has been exposed as a mediocre one over the past month, and the threat of the Chiefs simply running over this unit can't be exaggerated. Walker may get 25 carries if he's rolling, and if he's rolling, the Falcons are in major trouble.

Unfortunately for an Atlanta offense that has been a real puzzle box the past couple of weeks, the Kansas City defense is no pushover, either. They're doing a nice job of limiting explosive plays and are top ten in counting stats, success rate, and EPA defensively against the pass and the run, underscoring how fundamentally sound they are. A sloppy version of this Falcons offense is going to run into trouble, and a sloppy version of this Falcons offense is all we've gotten lately. Your best bet is going to be to run outside where the Chiefs are marginally less stout, and I'm sure Bijan Robinson has no problem with that plan. If everyone's blocking well, the Falcons should at least be able to get a toehold on the ground.

Things through the air are going to be tougher. We don't know how Corey Levin is going to hold up full-time as the starting center, but we do know it's a tough matchup against this Kansas City interior, and that Michael Penix Jr. has both struggled under pressure and faced way too many quick pressures in recent weeks. The Chiefs have the talent at every level to punish Penix locked in on receivers, Levin struggling against pressure, and Atlanta's pass catchers not getting separation. If the past two weeks of woes continue, this one could get out of hand.

One item to watch this week if the game is close? Atlanta has a new kicker and the Chiefs are sticking with Harrison Butker, who has been abysmal this season. If Jake Moody can keep rolling, as he has over the past couple of seasons, and Butker struggles, a 3-6 point game could swing on kickers.

Overall, though, I'm going to follow my own advice and urge you to do the same: Don't expect the Falcons to win a game against a superior opponent, and hope for the best.

U19 Euros Qualifiers: Winning start for Germany after late comeback against Sweden

📅 Nov 12, 2026  |  Category: Other  |  Read Source →

Germany started the second round of the U19 European Championship qualifying campaign with three points after their comeback victory over their Swedish counterparts. All the three goals of the game in Portugal were scored in the last 20 minutes.

The match was initially planned for Wednesday before it had to be postponed for the next day due to extreme weather. With the assistant coach Pascal Pellowski taking charge in place of the suspended Hanno Balistch, Germany U19 only had a single shot on target in the first 45 minutes.

Substitute Ahmed Saeed put Sweden in front from close range fifteen minutes before time. However, Germany found their way back in the game as two substitutes scored in the final five minutes of regular time.

Debutant Kevin Ekweribe tied the score after a cross from fellow Köln attacker Maik Akumu. Germany went on to push for the winner as Swedish goalkeeper John-Oliver Lacayo denied Noah Ringbeck a winning goal with a brilliant save.

Sweden failed to clear the resulting corner-kick from Mathieu Nguefack as Niklas Scheller completed the comeback from close range to secure a narrow victory for Germany in the opening game of Group A3. Last year's finalists will face hosts Portugal and Lithuania in the next five days as they now have a huge chance to advance for the Elite Round. Portugal beat Lithuania in the other game from the group as they also relied on a late goal.

Germany's victory was shadowed by another injury setback for Cassiano Kiala as the FC Bayern talent had to be subbed off early in the second-half. The center-back has only returned from a long-term injury in August.

The trilingual son of Mr Universe makes homecoming - NFL Gameday Guide

📅 Nov 12, 2026  |  Category: Sports  |  Read Source →

The NFL plays the eighth of its record nine international games in 2026 when the Detroit Lions face the New England Patriots at Bayern Munich's Allianz Arena, live on Sky Sports from 2pm Sunday; Kirk Cousins meanwhile makes his second debut for the Minnesota Vikings after his trade return

Amon-Ra St. Brown. The globe-trotting trilingual son of Mr Universe, named after an Egyptian god, returning for the most special of homecomings to his mother's native land. This time, as one of the stars of the NFL.

Some five years ago, St. Brown spoke to Sky Sports about the importance of his German heritage ahead of entering the NFL Draft. And now he gets to honour it by doing what he does best.

The Detroit Lions will play an international game for the first time since November 2015 this Sunday when they host the New England Patriots at Munich's Allianz Arena.

His mother Miriam hails from Leverkusen and predominantly spoke to the Lions wide receiver and his two brothers in German growing up, while also taking them back to Europe each summer to spend time with their grandparents and play soccer.

St Brown even represented the German national team alongside brother Osiris during his sophomore year of high school, and since landing in the NFL, has worn decals of the German flag on his helmet and returned to host football camps in the country.

St Brown sat his SATs in English, German and French, spent time in French academy Lycee International de Los Angeles, spent a semester in Paris and joined his mother in travelling across the world with trips to Spain, Italy, London, France, the Caribbean, his German home from home and beyond. It was his mother Miriam who revealed the Lions would be playing in Munich on a FaceTime call set up by the Lions back in February.

At long last, he gets to take centre stage overseas as the NFL reaches the final stop on its European tour with the eighth of a record nine international games in 2026.

St Brown brings one of the NFL's most fascinating backgrounds as a ready-made ambassador for the league's increasing global reach, which will be championed by way of its sixth regular-season matchup in Germany since 2022.

His own global connections travel as far as Egyptian nomenclature, after his father John - a three-time Mr World and two-time Mr Universe body-builder - named his son after Amun, the ancient Egyptian deity.

It is Germany the NFL can thank for one of its most gifted players, with St Brown's parents having met at a fitness trade show in Cologne in 1987.

His transatlantic ties have naturally forged a love of soccer as he pitches up in the home of Bayern Munich, where Ballon d'Or-winning England captain Harry Kane has often spoken of his own ambitions to step into the world of St. Brown - as an NFL kicker.

Has he got the leg to do it? Sure. Would it ever happen? Most would say no. Funnily enough, his age by the end of his football career - he is 33 now - is probably a primary reason above all as to why it wouldn't happen.

But will we take advantage of the opportunity to shoehorn Kane's name into a headline? Duh. Clicks pay for this writer's coffee (they don't).

"If I was going to do it, I would take it seriously. I would probably dedicate six months to a year to training," said Kane earlier this year. "Maybe if I am in the MLS in the future or somewhere like that it might be a thing where you could connect the two maybe with the seasons."

Regardless, the NFL's global ventures have often amounted to crossover collaboration between its travelling teams and the sporting giants hosting them; last week Atlanta Falcons running back Bijan Robinson caught up with Jude Bellingham ahead of their matchup with the Cincinnati Bengals at the Bernabeu, where the likes of Thibaut Courtois, Marc Cucurella and Raphael Varane were in attendance. As far as growing the game is concerned, it is a no-brainer of an easy win.

The Lions arrive 6-2 in a fiercely competitive NFC North, St. Brown continuing to be Jared Goff's prized weapon on the outside with a team-high 61 catches for 657 yards (10th most in the league) and five touchdowns within an offense ranked fifth in yards per game and fifth in scoring.

Detroit have won four straight games, including divisional victories over both the Minnesota Vikings and Green Bay Packers in the last three weeks. Running back Jahmyr Gibbs is also at the heart of their success with an NFL-high 1,132 yards and 11 touchdowns from scrimmage.

New England are 5-4 and coming off a 45-27 defeat to the Packers during which Drake Maye, struggling to live up to his MVP-contending heights of last season, threw three interceptions.

The Patriots are battling to swerve a Super Bowl hangover after February's one-sided defeat to the Seattle Seahawks and return to play in Germany for the second time after losing to the Indianapolis Colts in Frankfurt in 2023.

Please use Chrome browser for a more accessible video player

Germany remains one of the NFL's largest fanbases outside of North America, with millions of listeners having previously tuned in to the Armed Forces Network radio station based in Frankfurt during World War II.

The first broadcast out of AFN Frankfurt was on July 15, 1945 from a confiscated house, after which thousands of US troops remained stationed in Germany.

There were later five preseason NFL games in Germany from 1990 to 1994, while the country was also represented by the Hamburg Sea Devils, Rhein Fire, Berlin Thunder, Cologne Centurions and Frankfurt Galaxy in the former World League of American Football/NFL Europe/NFL Europa between 1991 and 2007.

Is there another player in football who reigns as his position's outright No 1 more so than Fred Warner? Arguably not.

The San Francisco 49ers stalwart has occupied top spot among inside linebackers for multiple years now, and remains an unrivalled difference-maker from his role within Raheem Morris' defense.

Warner has been electric in his first campaign back since suffering a season-ending injury in Week Six of 2025, racking up 77 total tackles, five forced fumbles, a fumble recovery, half a sack, 5.5 stuffs and three pass breakups. Few, if any, diagnose the game better than Warner, whose film study is as refined and as expertly-exercised as any player in football.

An incredible sequence against the Las Vegas Raiders last week saw him punch the ball from the hands of Brock Bowers, recover Michael Mayers' fumble in the end zone and deny Kirk Cousins on a quarterback sneak with an incredible leaping stop over center Tyler Linderbaum in the space of about 10 minutes.

Wonder why teams are scared to throw over the middle of the field against the 49ers? Fred Warner.

Please use Chrome browser for a more accessible video player

The Minnesota Vikings would be Super Bowl contenders if they could just figure things out at quarterback, and on Tuesday they rolled the dice once more by trading to bring back Kirk Cousins.

Any scenario in which Justin Jefferson isn't getting the ball thrown his way becomes a major concern; the Vikings had no choice. Kyler Murray could not get it done, the Vikings traded away J.J. McCarthy to the New York Giants and on Monday night Carson Wentz epitomised this hobbling offense, which has failed miserably in its bid to back up Brian Flores' defense.

Cousins may be 38 years old, but has been impressive for the Las Vegas Raiders this season and gives Minnesota a significantly better chance of reigniting Jefferson, who has been starved of production.

Cousins is 202 of 315, passing for 2,340 yards, 20 touchdowns, eight interceptions, and a passer rating of 97.1 as he re-joins the franchise he played for from 2018-2023. Would be quite the story, huh?

Please use Chrome browser for a more accessible video player

Jordan Love is flying, and it feels as though the Green Bay Packers are gathering Super Bowl momentum by the week as their offense begins to takeover.

Love now leads the NFL in passing yards with 2,618 for 19 touchdowns to six interceptions as the league's most aggressive splash-play merchant.

He ranks first in intended air yards (3,251), intended air yards per attempt (9.5) and completed air yards (1,553) at the heart of the league's second-best passing attack, within which his receivers have notably dropped a league-high 23 passes.

They have become box office viewing, with two No 1 receivers emerging in Christian Watson - third in the league with 879 yards - and Matthew Golden - fourth in the league with 783 yards.

Matt LaFleur has typically built his offense around run game foundations that he can tee off from through the air, but even through the NFL's worst rushing attack he is finding success thanks to Love's arm.

Please use Chrome browser for a more accessible video player

Sunday Night Football stages one of the biggest games of the weekend when the Cincinnati Bengals take on the Pittsburgh Steelers in a possible AFC North division decider.

Both sit 6-2 with the Steelers holding the head-to-head advantage after their 30-27 win in the reverse fixture back in September, Jaylen Warren starring out of the backfield on that occasion with 176 yards from scrimmage.

But the Bengals have hit their stride with a four-game winning streak over the last month, Joe Burrow rising to MVP contention while playing some of the best football of his NFL career within the league's most potent passing attack.

Across that four-game span, Burrow has completed 77 per cent of passes for 1,215 yards with 18 total touchdowns, three interceptions and a 132.0 passer rating.

Cincinnati look like legitimate Super Bowl contenders again, producing their best start through eight games since 2015.

Please use Chrome browser for a more accessible video player

The 'super-team' Los Angeles Rams are not looking so super, and there is growing reason to be concerned over their playoff chances this season.

Sean McVay's side were dealt another crushing blow this week when it was revealed Puka Nacua had suffered a right knee injury set to land him on injured reserve and miss at least a month of action, adding to an absentee list already featuring Davante Adams.

Rams General Manager Les Snead is typically one of football's most aggressive dealers, but on Tuesday neglected to add receiver help for Matthew Stafford as they enter Sunday's divisional clash against the Arizona Cardinals 5-4.

McVay is known for his multiple tight end sets, and will now need to lean on as much alongside Kyren Williams and Blake Corum in order to keep this offense afloat

The Best Rain Jackets for Hikers Who Always Overheat

📅 Nov 12, 2026  |  Category: Business  |  Read Source →

If you buy through our links, we may earn an affiliate commission. This supports our mission to get more people active and outside.Learn about Outside Online's affiliate link policy

Picture this: You’re halfway through a 20-mile day when the sky opens up, dumping sheets of rain on you and your hiking partners. You pull out your shell, zip it up, and keep crushing. An hour later, you notice you’re starting to feel awfully damp. Your shell isn’t leaking, but it might as well be: Your exertion has turned it into a sweatbox.

The best rain jackets are next to useless if you end up sweating them out. If you’re the kind of hiker who always steams up your shell, picking one that has the breathability and venting options to keep you cool and dry is a must. We went through our testers’ favorite rain shells of 2026 and chose the best picks for hikers who always overheat.

Weight: 15.3 oz (men’s) / 13.9 oz (women’s) Sizes: Men’s S-XXXL / Women’s XXS-4X

Pros and Cons + Very breathable + Slightly stretchy + Certified carbon neutral – Too slim for layering

Our favorite rain jacket on the market today is also the most breathable we’ve tested: After wearing the Aspire (the Foray is the men’s equivalent) on a steep, 7-mile hike into a cabin in Alaska’s Chugach National Forest, tester Kate Paskievitch reported that she stayed dry the entire time—even with 40 pounds on her back. Outdoor Research’s AscentShell Dry, a 40-denier, 100-percent recycled three-layer nylon fabric, helped, but credit mostly goes to the full-length side zippers, which helped us bleed off heat quickly.

Weight: 10.9 oz (men’s) / 9.9 oz (women’s) Sizes: Men’s XS-XXL / Women’s XS-XXL

Pros and Cons + Excellent adjustability + Superior breathability + Dialed-in details – Expensive

Let’s acknowledge the elephant in the room first: Any rain jacket that costs $600 had better be really good. Fortunately, this model from Canada-based 7mesh delivers. Diagonal pit zips kept us from sweating out the Guardian Apex, even when our testers’ arms were down. And the nylon face fabric was both surprisingly breathable and tough enough that it remained snag-free, even after a long bushwhack through brambles.

Weight: 5.8 oz (men’s) / 5.5 oz (women’s) Sizes: Men’s S-XXL / Women’s XS-XL

Pros and Cons + Very light and packable + Partially plant-based – No hand pockets

At just 5.8 ounces for a men’s medium—roughly the same as the ever-popular Frogg Toggs Ultra Lite—the Marmot SuperAlloy Bio takes up barely any space, making it an ideal just-in-case piece to tuck into your pack. And it performs, too: The 2.5-layer NanoPro fabric kept our tester dry even after she wore it under a heavy pack in 50-degree temperatures, but was still tough enough that it didn’t fray after heavy use.

Weight: 25.4 oz (men’s) / 25.0 oz (women’s) Sizes: Men’s S-XXL / Women’s XS-3XL

Pros and Cons + Soft and supple + Lots of venting options + Easy to repair and actually recyclable – Heavy and bulky – Too warm for summer weather

The Velez Adventure Smock is not the lightest jacket in our test: The jacket, which eschews a waterproof-breathable membrane for a DWR-treated polyester face backed by wicking fabric, is better suited for colder-season temperatures. So what makes it a good jacket for hikers who run hot? In shoulder-season conditions, it breathes and maintains its waterproofing better than most traditional three-layer rain shells. Venting helps, too: Biceps zippers and a horizontal front flap across the torso make it easy to dump heat quickly before users get too steamy.

Weight: 1.8 oz (men’s) Sizes: Men’s XS-XXL

Pros and Cons + Ultralight + Surprisingly durable + Surprisingly weatherproof – No hood or chest pocket – Tight fit

At 1.8 ounces, this gossamer hoodless jacket is a reliable friend for mountain runners, fastpackers, and anyone who values velocity. The 10-denier recycled nylon exterior packs up to (literally) pocket size, and while we wouldn’t bet on it to keep you bone-dry through a multi-day storm, it handled surprise desert downpours without batting an eye. Breathability is exceptional, which our tester confirmed after a full day of biking along the Grand Canyon’s South Rim.

First, understand you may have to compromise. While waterproof-breathable membranes are almost ubiquitous, most jackets prioritize waterproofing over breathability, or vice versa. Jackets that can weather a downpour without so much as wetting out may not breathe well, and those that breathe well may be better for scattered showers than the kind of rain that makes you start thinking about building an ark.

Pay attention not just to the breathability of the jacket itself, but to the venting options, too. Even the most breathable fabrics can’t dump heat as well as a set of pit zips or mesh-backed pockets that expose your underlayers to fresh air. Packability is more important than you might think, too: If you’re consistently hot, having a jacket that you can ball up and stick inside your pack without a second thought is a better move than carrying a heavy, bulky one.

Our lead testers for men’s and women’s rain jackets are Drew Zieff and Elizabeth Kwak. Our gear editors pulled these recommendations from their original testing notes and scores to recommend the best rain jackets for hikers who overheat.

Here's what Nvidia interim CEO Colette Kress and board chair Harvey Jones wrote to Nvidia employees

📅 2026-11-12  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

On the heels of founder Jensen Huang's resignation as CEO of Nvidia, new interim CEO Colette Kress and board chair Harvey Jones sent emails to employees Thursday expressing gratitude and optimism for the future.

CNBC obtained copies of the emails from a source familiar with the matter, who said Jones sent the first email and Kress replied shortly after.

Follow CNBC's live coverage of Nvidia CEO Jensen Huang's resignation

"The Board, Estaff and I recognize that this has been an extraordinarily difficult period and I want you to know how grateful we are for your resilience, professionalism and continued dedication," Kress wrote in her email.

The term Estaff is commonly used to refer to executive staff, the group of direct reports to C-suite executives at a company. Nvidia will host a company meeting on November 18 at 9am PST, her email said.

Board chair Jones took a similar tone in his email, and praised Kress as "a key architect of NVIDIA's growth and capital strategy."

"The Board has full confidence in Colette to deliver on our commitments to shareholders, customers, partners, and to each of you," Jones wrote.

Below are the staff emails obtained by CNBC on Thursday.

I believe deeply in this company and in its future. More than anything, I believe in each of you, in our customers and partners and in the vital role we play every day in the global economy.

The Board, Estaff and I recognize that this has been an extraordinarily difficult period and I want you to know how grateful we are for your resilience, professionalism and continued dedication. Thank you.

This company has achieved so much because of the work that all of you bring to it every single day. You are our greatest strength. You will hear more from leadership in the coming days about our future together, and we will host a company meeting on November 18 at 9am PT.

We play a vital role in building the technology that is powering the AI revolution, and we have important work ahead of us. So let's move forward together, with the same determination and commitment that have always defined this company.

I am writing to share an important update. Jensen has resigned as CEO and Board Member, and the Board has asked our Chief Financial Officer Colette Kress to serve as interim CEO while it conducts a search for a permanent successor looking at both internal and external candidates. I will serve as the first-ever Chair of the Board.

As CFO for over 13 years, Colette has been a key architect of NVIDIA's growth and capital strategy. She possesses a deep understanding of this business and the markets in which we operate. The Board has full confidence in Colette to deliver on our commitments to shareholders, customers, partners, and to each of you.

Jensen's contributions to NVIDIA are profound. NVIDIA is one of the most consequential technology companies in the world. We pioneered accelerated computing, defined the modern GPU, and built the platform that powered artificial intelligence.

The Board takes seriously our duty as stewards of this great company.

The strength of this company resides in our employees, leadership team, technical strength and partner ecosystem. I am confident that under Colette's leadership, with the full support of the Board, we will continue to grow our business, and lead the industry forward. Thank you for your focus, your professionalism, and your commitment to NVIDIA.

Harvey JonesChair, NVIDIA Board of Directors

World Of Hyatt Brand Explorer: Free Night Awards For Staying At New Brands

📅 Nov 12, 2026  |  Category: World  |  Read Source →

There are several ways to earn World of Hyatt free night award awards. In this post, I’d like to talk about one of the often overlooked ways to earn Hyatt free nights, which is with World of Hyatt’s ongoing Brand Explorer promotion.

This offers World of Hyatt members the opportunity to earn free night awards for exploring Hyatt’s portfolio. Given the huge brand inflation that Hyatt has seen in recent years, this can potentially offer up to seven free night awards, and counting.

With World of Hyatt’s Brand Explorer promotion, you can earn a Category 1-4 free night certificate for every five unique Hyatt brands you stay at. When it comes to earning Hyatt Brand Explorer free night awards, here’s what you should know:

If you want to see how many Hyatt brands you’ve stayed at, you can do so either through the World of Hyatt website or mobile app. Just go to the “Brand Explorer” section, and there will be a display showing how many unique brands you’ve stayed at (including which ones), and also how many free night certificates you’ve earned.

When it comes to redeeming Hyatt Brand Explorer free night awards, here’s what you should know:

Of course you’ll maximize value with Brand Explorer by redeeming your free night award at an eligible Hyatt property when the cash rate would be as high as possible. World of Hyatt Category 4 properties can cost as much as 25,000 points per night, and in some situations, could retail for over $300 per night. So if you can find a hotel anywhere in that range, it’s a great way to maximize value.

Hyatt currently has 38 brands, which are as follows:

So as you can see, you’d need to stay at 35 brands to earn seven free night awards. If/when Hyatt adds two more brands, it’ll be possible to earn eight free night awards.

The World of Hyatt Brand Explorer offer is a fun way for the program to engage members and try to get them to try more brands. At least in one case it impacted where I chose to stay, because I do love checking this dashboard every so often, both to see the progress that I’m making, and be reminded of all the brands I’ve stayed at.

Even though I’ve had top tier status with World of Hyatt for over a decade and have now earned lifetime Globalist status, I haven’t even stayed at half of Hyatt’s current brand portfolio. As a matter of fact, I’ve only stayed at 13 of the 38 brands, so that’s just over one-third of properties.

Looking at the Hyatt brands I haven’t stayed at, most of them are all-inclusives, as that’s an area where Hyatt is growing significantly. However, in general this has limited appeal to me, based on the type of travel that I do. I’m sure I’ll knock out a few more brands in the coming years — maybe I’ll get to 15 brands, though I doubt I’ll make it to 20 brands in the foreseeable future.

With World of Hyatt’s ongoing Brand Explorer promotion, you can earn a free Category 1-4 free night awards for every five Hyatt brands you stay at. This is a way to earn up to seven free nights over time, based on Hyatt’s current portfolio of 38 brands. As Hyatt’s portfolio continues to grow, the opportunities to earn free nights continues to increase.

However, staying at Hyatt’s all-inclusive properties requires some effort, given that they’re not typically places you just drop into for a night.

Given how easy Hyatt makes it to track this, I’m curious — how many Hyatt brands have you stayed at? Which OMAAT reader has the record for the most brands?

The ‘Campus Drivers’ Finale May Have Revealed Who Season 2 Will Be About, and It’s a Change From the Books

📅 Nov 12, 2026  |  Category: Other  |  Read Source →

Lois and Lane’s unfinished story could signal a major shift.

The season 1 finale of Campus Drivers left viewers with several questions, one of them being: Who will the protagonists of the next season be? Prime Video’s French-language series is based on the book series by C.S. Quill, but it has already demonstrated that it doesn’t necessarily intend to follow the novels’ path to the letter. And the very way Lane and Lois’s story ends could foreshadow an important narrative choice for the future of the adaptation.

As a reminder: In the series finale, Lane decides to leave for Los Angeles with his mother in order to allow Lois to keep her scholarship. Their story is cut short just when it seemed destined to turn into something more, leaving several questions open that could become central in a second season.

However, season 1 does also end with a twist involving Donovan. Without giving too much away about what happens in the second book, Crash Course, which is centered on Donovan, the ending sees (spoiler!) Donovan’s dad collapse during a basketball practice. Still, Lois and Lane remain at the center of the narrative with a story still entirely unresolved…and this choice may not be accidental.

The choice to keep Lane and Lois at the center of the story could suggest that Campus Drivers will not necessarily follow the narrative model of Off Campus. In the series based on Elle Kennedy’s novels, each season is designed to mainly tell the story of a new couple, giving space to different protagonists within the same narrative universe.

For Campus Drivers (despite the series having the same structure), the direction could be different: keeping Lane and Lois as central figures in the coming seasons as well, gradually developing the love stories of the other characters and giving them more and more space. Based on the season 1 ending, it’s possible that it will instead use an increasingly ensemble structure, in which the protagonists’ stories intertwine and become richer episode after episode, without necessarily making way for a new main couple each season.

This does not rule out the possibility that, further down the line, other characters could take on an even more important role. As early as the third season, for example, there could be room to explore the secondary love stories in greater depth. For now, however, these are only hypotheses; Prime Video has not yet confirmed what the structure of any future chapters will be.

If the series is renewed, it is reasonable to expect Lane and Lois to maintain a central role. The first season finale left their relationship hanging, and it would be difficult to imagine a follow-up that does not pick up precisely from what happened between them—especially because six episodes felt like too few to fully develop a dynamic between the leads.

The choice not to follow the structure of the novels could also be a way to differentiate Campus Drivers from Off Campus, especially since both series share the same platform and target the same audience of fans.

At the moment, a second season has not yet been officially confirmed. However, considering the attention the series is receiving and the narrative potential left open by the finale, it would not be surprising to see new updates arrive soon.

Bengals designate Andrei Iosivas, two others for return from IR

📅 Nov 12, 2026  |  Category: Entertainment  |  Read Source →

The Bengals will have three players back at practice on Thursday.

They announced that they have designated wide receiver Andrei Iosivas, cornerback Jalen Davis, and guard Brian Parker for return from injured reserve. All three players will be able to practice with the team for the next 21 days and they can return to the active roster at any point in that window.

Iosivas has been out since Week 2 with a thumb injury. He had three catches for 19 yards in the first two weeks of the season and had 69 catches over the previous two seasons.

Davis played in the first three games and has been out with a hamstring injury. He had five tackles, a tackle for loss and a pass defensed before his injury.

Parker was a sixth-round pick this year and landed on injured reserve ahead of Week 1.

Hyatt Hotel Promises Jonathan Adler Toiletries—But There’s Drugstore Shampoo On Its Housekeeping Cart

📅 Nov 12, 2026  |  Category: Business  |  Read Source →

Hotel Avante in Mountain View, California advertises Jonathan Adler bath amenities. A reader photographed its housekeeping cart with a retail bottle of HASK shampoo resting among unbranded squeeze bottles. This is another JdV by Hyatt property, just a few miles from the Wild Palms hotel where I reported on DRIFT bulk toiletries on housekeeping carts while the guest bathroom bottles displayed Jonathan Adler branding. Apparently readers need to keep checking what’s on the carts. The brown bottle is HASK Tea Tree Oil & Rosemary Invigorating Shampoo, in the 12-ounce size. HASK is an affordable retail hair care brand, with products sold at CVS and other stores. Around it are condiment-style squeeze bottles. At least one has “Body wash” handwritten on tape. That identifies the type of product, without telling guests whose product it is. It seems like Hyatt a good reason to check what this hotel is using to refill its toiletries. But they had this reason at its sister property, too. There needs to be much stronger monitoring. Hyatt’s Hotel Avante page lists “Jonathan Adler bath amenities” across all six room categories. The hotel’s separate website promises that as well. I have no particular opinion on HASK shampoo. My issue is that a hotel should deliver the product they specifically advertise to guests. And if the bulk toiletries in the room say they’re a specific brand, they should be that brand. Sures describes Avante as Wild Palms’ sister property and they’re both part of the same ownership group. In November 2025, I published a reader’s photos from Wild Palms. The bathroom had Jonathan Adler bottles, while the housekeeping cart carried large jugs of DRIFT Hydrated Body Care. Then in January, Ben at One Mile at a Time published follow-up photos showing the bulk jugs with their labels removed. Wild Palms still advertises Jonathan Adler toiletries. I just wrote about the ketchup, mustard and mayonnaise bottle approach to hotel toiletries. Once the hotel keeps reusing the branded packaging, it can change the contents without changing anything the guest sees. An owner who substitutes a cheaper product keeps the savings, while Hyatt and the advertised toiletry company risk their reputations. Remind me again why bulk dispensers were a better idea than single use biodegradable packaging? This is another JdV by Hyatt property, just a few miles from the Wild Palms hotel where I reported on DRIFT bulk toiletries on housekeeping carts while the guest bathroom bottles displayed Jonathan Adler branding. Apparently readers need to keep checking what’s on the carts.

The brown bottle is HASK Tea Tree Oil & Rosemary Invigorating Shampoo, in the 12-ounce size. HASK is an affordable retail hair care brand, with products sold at CVS and other stores. Around it are condiment-style squeeze bottles. At least one has “Body wash” handwritten on tape. That identifies the type of product, without telling guests whose product it is.

It seems like Hyatt a good reason to check what this hotel is using to refill its toiletries. But they had this reason at its sister property, too. There needs to be much stronger monitoring.

Hyatt’s Hotel Avante page lists “Jonathan Adler bath amenities” across all six room categories. The hotel’s separate website promises that as well.

I have no particular opinion on HASK shampoo. My issue is that a hotel should deliver the product they specifically advertise to guests. And if the bulk toiletries in the room say they’re a specific brand, they should be that brand.

Sures describes Avante as Wild Palms’ sister property and they’re both part of the same ownership group. In November 2025, I published a reader’s photos from Wild Palms. The bathroom had Jonathan Adler bottles, while the housekeeping cart carried large jugs of DRIFT Hydrated Body Care.

Then in January, Ben at One Mile at a Time published follow-up photos showing the bulk jugs with their labels removed. Wild Palms still advertises Jonathan Adler toiletries.

I just wrote about the ketchup, mustard and mayonnaise bottle approach to hotel toiletries. Once the hotel keeps reusing the branded packaging, it can change the contents without changing anything the guest sees. An owner who substitutes a cheaper product keeps the savings, while Hyatt and the advertised toiletry company risk their reputations. Remind me again why bulk dispensers were a better idea than single use biodegradable packaging?

Lions vs. Patriots matches up this year's two most aggressive head coaches on fourth downs

📅 Nov 12, 2026  |  Category: Entertainment  |  Read Source →

When the Lions meet the Patriots in Munich on Sunday, the fans in Germany are likely to see both teams go for it on fourth down.

That’s because Lions head coach Dan Campbell has long been the league’s most aggressive coach on fourth downs, and this year, Patriots coach Mike Vrabel is No. 2.

Coaches’ fourth down decisions are tracked for the Aggressiveness Index at FTN Fantasy, looking at how often coaches go for it compared to other coaches in the same situation. By that measurement, Campbell is first with a score of 1.45 (meaning he goes for it 45 percent more than an average NFL coach) and Vrabel is second with a score of 1.22.

The Aggressiveness Index is a more detailed way of tracking a coach’s fourth-down decision making than simply looking at the number of times a team has gone for it on fourth down. Some teams go for it often on fourth down not because the coach is aggressive, but because they happen to face a lot of fourth-and-shorts while trailing late in the fourth quarter. That’s not a particularly aggressive time to go for it.

What gives Campbell such a high score on the Aggressiveness Index is decisions like going for it on fourth down at his own 24-yard line with the score tied late in the fourth quarter against the Packers. That’s the kind of call that Campbell makes and other coaches don’t, which is why his Aggressiveness Index is so much higher than any other coach’s.

Campbell is at or near the top of the Aggressiveness Index every year. Last year he was second only to Mike Kafka, who was very aggressive on fourth downs during his seven-game stint as the Giants’ interim head coach. Perhaps unsurprisingly, after Kafka was not retained by the Giants, Campbell hired him to be the Lions’ passing game coordinator.

Vrabel has not been as aggressive as Campbell on fourth downs, but he has been more aggressive than the average NFL coach, both during his time with the Patriots and before that as head coach of the Titans. Both Vrabel and Campbell are former players with reputations for a blend of old-school toughness and a new-school understanding of the strategic advantages of aggressive fourth-down decision making. It’s a winning combination.

Measles outbreaks in 2026: the key numbers from the CDC's case tracker

📅 Nov 12, 2026  |  Category: Business  |  Read Source →

Free daily briefing on global business news.

The CDC counted 5,182 U.S. measles cases by Nov. 5, 2026. Nine key numbers show who is sick, who is hospitalized and what is at stake.

Most Americans think of measles as a disease from an earlier era, one that vaccines dealt with long before their own children started school. For most of this century, the record supported that view. Between 2000 and 2024, the worst year was 2019, with 1,282 cases by one count from the CDC, although the agency's figures for that year differ slightly from page to page. Even that peak was small for a nation this size, and it showed measles as a rare, contained event until very recently. Against that baseline, the past two years stand out. Case counts climbed quickly, and they have not fallen back to the old level. The 2026 numbers keep raising one question: whether the country is living through a bad stretch or watching measles settle back in to stay.

More is at stake than a grim tally, because the country's standing as a place where measles no longer circulates is under formal review. A decision from the Pan American Health Organization on that elimination status is expected next week, according to Reuters. NBC 26 reports that Canada lost its own status last year. For the U.S., losing the status is a near and concrete possibility with a precedent next door. No ruling has been issued, so nothing is lost yet. The open question is whether the numbers already show the country failing the test. Whatever the ruling, those numbers show how far protection has slipped in parts of the country. That slippage matters more to families than any official label, because it decides where measles can spread next.

The Centers for Disease Control and Prevention built its measles tracker to show how large U.S. measles activity was in 2026 and what that activity was made of. Its figures show who is catching the disease, how many patients end up in the hospital, and how far vaccination among kindergartners has slipped. Read together, the figures do more than tally a bad year. They trace measles through the people left without protection, and the slides that follow pick up that thread.

The U.S. has recorded more measles cases in 2026 than in all of 2025, with about two months of the year still to count. Health officials confirmed 5,182 cases by Nov. 5, more than double the total for all of last year, according to the Centers for Disease Control and Prevention. Officials reached that mark before the year was out, while the 2025 figure covers a full 12 months. They can only add cases from here, which makes "more than double" the minimum for the year rather than its final result.

A large part of that record comes from just two states. Pennsylvania has counted 1,487 cases, according to state health department figures reported by Reuters, and South Carolina has recorded 670. Pennsylvania's count comes from the state rather than the CDC, but it places a large part of the national record inside a single state. Together, those two states carry a major share of this year's cases, and the rise has hit some parts of the country far harder than others.

Almost everyone who caught measles in 2026 lives in the U.S. International visitors account for only 18 of the more than 5,000 confirmed cases. Measles is easy to picture as something travelers bring in from abroad, but this year's cases come from outbreaks inside American communities.

For anyone weighing their own risk, gaps in local vaccination matter far more than who crosses the border.

Nearly every measles case in the U.S. this year has been tied to an outbreak. According to the Centers for Disease Control and Prevention, 96% of 2026 cases were linked to outbreaks, and 43 new outbreaks began during the year. The isolated case, in which one person picks up the virus and no one else gets sick, has almost vanished from the count. In a year driven by outbreaks, the danger is concentrated in the communities where a cluster has formed, while cases elsewhere remain rare.

Fewer new outbreaks began in 2026 than in 2025, when there were 48, yet this year's case count still climbed past last year's. Part of the explanation is that some outbreaks never ended. This year's count includes 1,385 cases from outbreaks that began in 2025, so a large share of the 2026 record was already underway before the year started. The record reflects outbreaks that kept going from last year as well as the ones that started this year. When the same chain of infection continues from one calendar year into the next, measles has become a lasting problem in those communities rather than a passing one.

Health officials in each state now hold the most current view of these outbreaks and run the response to them. The national count is a running total for the year, and the CDC itself says state health departments keep more current figures on the outbreaks within their borders. Pennsylvania is home to the country's biggest outbreak, according to Contagion Live. In New York, Gov. Kathy Hochul responded in October by declaring a disaster emergency for the state. Anyone living close to one of these clusters should check with their own state health department for the newest numbers.

Most people picture measles as a childhood illness that passes through classrooms and daycare rooms. In 2026, though, adults accounted for as much of it as school-age children did. Adults 20 and older and children ages 5 to 19 each made up 41% of confirmed cases through Nov. 5, according to preliminary figures from the Centers for Disease Control and Prevention, and children under 5 were the smallest group at 18%. Measles this year is not mainly a worry for parents of young children, and grown-ups make up as large a part of the spread as students do.

The large adult share came late in the year. Adults made up 29% of cases across all of 2025. Their share was 30% in July 2026, according to HealthDay. For the adult share of the full year to reach 41% by November, the cases recorded from summer onward must have involved adults more often than the earlier ones did. Any single age breakdown is a snapshot, and the groups that see new cases can keep changing as long as transmission continues. That shifting profile is a sign of a disease that has settled in, not an outbreak that comes and goes.

The wide range of ages among patients is evidence of a gap in protection that opened over many years, not in a single season. "That tells us this has been years of undervaccination in some communities," Dr. William Moss, a professor of epidemiology at the Johns Hopkins Bloomberg School of Public Health, told HealthDay. The people left unprotected over those years now include older children and adults, not just the newest group of kids.

In communities where vaccination rates have fallen, no age group is safe, and the question that matters is how well each community kept up its vaccinations.

This year's record measles count does not mean the vaccine has stopped working. The Centers for Disease Control and Prevention reports that 96% of 2026 cases were in people who were unvaccinated or whose vaccination status was unknown. Only 4% of patients were known to have been vaccinated, according to NBC 26. The disease is spreading among people who never got the shot or whose protection nobody can confirm, and it is rarely breaking through the vaccine.

People who finished the full two-dose course barely appear in this year's count, making up just 2% of 2026 cases. For a family deciding on the shot, completing both doses puts a child in the group that has stayed almost entirely out of this year's outbreaks.

The same lopsided split held through last year's outbreaks. In 2025, 93% of cases were unvaccinated or of unknown status. This year's share is higher still, so cases have stayed concentrated among the unprotected for two straight years, and measles has not moved into the vaccinated population in any meaningful way. Two years of the same pattern point to gaps in vaccination, not a weakness in the vaccine, as the reason measles keeps spreading.

The 2026 case count amounts to a tally of the people that vaccination has not reached.

Hospitals have treated far more measles patients this year, even though a person who catches the disease is no more likely to need a hospital than a year ago. About 11% of this year's cases needed hospital care, the same share as in 2025, according to the Centers for Disease Control and Prevention. The jump in hospital patients comes from the jump in cases, with 562 measles patients hospitalized so far this year, up from 244 in all of 2025. A steady rate can sound like reassurance. With an outbreak that keeps growing, the same risk still means more patients in hospital beds every month the spread continues.

For someone with no vaccine protection, the chance of a hospital stay is much higher than 11%. Dr. Dennis Wang, an Epidemic Intelligence Service officer at the CDC, and his colleagues report that approximately 20% of unvaccinated U.S. measles patients were hospitalized. The 11% figure counts everyone who caught the virus, including people with some protection and mild cases that may barely register. That is why the national figure can be lower than the risk facing one unprotected child. The unchanged national rate is not a sign that measles is getting milder. It reflects this year's mix of patients, not a fixed trait of the disease.

The patients who did end up in the hospital were often seriously ill. In the west Texas outbreak, most of the hospitalized patients whose records were reviewed needed extra oxygen, according to the CDC. None of those patients was admitted only to keep the virus from spreading to others. Each of those hospital stays reflected the patient's own illness.

The west Texas records cover only part of one outbreak, but there, at least, a hospital bed went to a patient who was seriously ill.

School-age children are the least likely to end up in the hospital with measles, and the odds rise sharply at both ends of the age range. This year 17% of infected children under 5 and 15% of infected adults were hospitalized, compared with 4% of school-age children, according to the Centers for Disease Control and Prevention. For parents, a preschooler with measles faces far higher odds of a hospital stay than a 10-year-old does, and grown-ups face odds nearly as high as toddlers. In west Texas, the most serious hospital stays involved children. The CDC reports that all four hospitalized patients admitted to intensive care in the early months of that outbreak were under 18. The number is small, but no hospitalized adult in that period needed that level of care.

The adult rate deserves close attention, because adults make up a growing share of patients the longer an outbreak lasts. By the end of the west Texas outbreak, adults accounted for 32.4% of all cases, according to the CDC. Because hospital stays are common among infected adults, a long outbreak moves many more people into an age group that often needs hospital care. If this year's outbreaks keep running, more adults may end up in the hospital, although that remains a possibility rather than a measured trend.

Some adult hospital stays may reflect caution rather than severe illness. The CDC reports that four of the five adults hospitalized in the early months of the west Texas outbreak were women in the third trimester of pregnancy, and that none of those women developed pneumonia or low oxygen levels. Part of the adult hospital rate may therefore come from precautionary care during pregnancy rather than from measles at its worst, though that evidence rests on a handful of patients in a single outbreak.

The very young are hospitalized most often and adults nearly as often, but in west Texas the patients who needed intensive care were all children.

More Americans have died of measles this year than in all of 2025, and the year still has weeks to run. The CDC counts four measles deaths in 2026 as of Nov. 5, compared with three for the whole of last year. Even before December arrives, 2026 is already the deadlier year. "This number is subject to change; CDC will update its reporting as additional information becomes available and relevant reviews are completed," the CDC said. Readers should treat four as the least that has happened so far rather than as a settled total.

The number of measles deaths this year depends on who does the counting. Pennsylvania announced a measles death on Sept. 30 that brought the nation's toll to five, HealthDay reports, while the national count at the time listed two because the CDC uses its own rule to decide which deaths to include. State announcements and the federal tally can therefore give different totals for the same year. Readers should expect the federal figure to sit below what states report.

The deaths have come from the places where outbreaks have grown largest. Pennsylvania, where the most recent death was announced, has the largest active outbreak in the U.S., according to HealthDay. While outbreaks of that size remain active, the toll remains open.

The number to watch is not whether the count rises, but where it rises next.

Fewer kindergartners are vaccinated against measles than the level needed to keep the disease from spreading. The CDC reports that 92.4% of children starting school in the 2025–2026 school year had their MMR shots, down from 95.2% in 2019–2020. Protecting a community from measles takes coverage above 95%, according to the CDC. In about six years, the country went from just above that line to below it. A shortfall of a few points matters with measles because the protective line is set so high, and even a small gap leaves enough unprotected children for one case to lead to others. The gap is large in human terms: approximately 280,000 children in kindergarten classes during 2025–2026 were at risk.

The national rate still understates the danger, because people catch measles from those around them, and vaccination levels differ from one community to the next. "At local levels, vaccine coverage rates may vary considerably, and pockets of unvaccinated people can exist in states with high vaccination coverage," the CDC said. The CDC lists lower kindergarten coverage and rising measles activity around the world as the reasons for the increase in cases. Lower coverage at home does not cause the cases that arrive from abroad. It determines whether one of those cases stays isolated or leads to an outbreak, depending on how many people near the patient are vaccinated. Large outbreaks can therefore happen despite only a modest national decline, wherever unvaccinated children are grouped in particular schools and towns.

For parents, the vaccination rate at their own child's school tells them more than the national figure does.

Measles did not drop out of American life on its own. Public health officials declared it eliminated because Americans vaccinated their children year after year until the virus could no longer circulate. The CDC reports that the vaccine was licensed in 1963 and that elimination was declared in 2000, after decades of vaccination. The CDC lists three conditions behind that result: an effective vaccine, high vaccination coverage among children and a strong system for responding to cases. The country earned the status by keeping enough children protected, not because the disease vanished from the world. Because elimination depends on that coverage, the country can lose it the same way it won it.

"Eliminated" sounds final, but the standard behind the word is narrow. A country keeps the label as long as measles has not spread continuously within its borders for a year or longer. A country can record imported cases and even outbreaks and still qualify, as long as each chain of spread ends within a year. A high case count alone does not decide the question. The answer depends on whether any chain of infection in the U.S. now runs unbroken for a year. That puts the weight on the outbreaks that carried over from 2025 rather than on the size of this year's total.

The weak point is how many people get the vaccine, not how well it works. Two MMR doses are about 97% effective, the CDC reports, and nearly everyone who receives both doses stays protected. Too few children are getting their shots across much of the country, and in 22 states fewer than 90% of children born in 2021 have received the MMR vaccine. Coverage was one of the three conditions behind elimination, and as it weakens, the year-long chains of infection that the standard rules out have more room to form.

Travelers $TRV will keep bringing measles in from abroad whatever the ruling, so the country's standing was always going to depend on how many people at home stay vaccinated.

Broncos announce trade for Chargers LB Daiyan Henley falls through, 'will not be finalized'

📅 Nov 12, 2026  |  Category: Sports  |  Read Source →

If not listed, please contact your TV provider.

The Denver Broncos will not be upgrading their linebacker room after all. The Broncos announced on social media late Wednesday night that "[t]he proposed trade involving Chargers linebacker Daiyan Henley will not be finalized."

Denver had agreed to send a 2027 fourth-round pick and a 2028 fifth-round pick to Los Angeles in exchange for Henley on deadline day. The Broncos did not provide a reason why the trade is not being finalized.

According to ESPN, Denver had concerns about Henley following his physical. When the trade was initially announced, the Broncos said it was "pending a physical." Even after said physical, per ESPN, the Broncos wanted the trade to go through and discussed revised terms with the Chargers, but no agreement was reached.

Henley has played all eight games for the Chargers this season, recording 51 tackles, two tackles for loss, three quarterback hits, half a sack, two passes defensed, one forced fumble and two fumble recoveries. He's in the final season of his rookie contract and set to become an unrestricted free agent in 2027.

The Broncos will move forward with Alex Singleton and Justin Strnad as their primary linebackers, as they have been for the entire season to date. They still have one of the top defenses in the NFL, and they're still in first place in the AFC West. But they will not get the lift in the middle of their defense that they expected to get when they agreed to this trade in the first place.

On deadline day, CBS Sports gave the Broncos an 'A-' for this deal, given the help Henley would provide. Here's what writer Zachary Pereles initially said:

"The Broncos quietly made a helpful deal, acquiring Chargers linebacker Daiyan Henley for a couple of mid-round picks. Henley has plateaued a bit the past couple of seasons after a strong start, but he's an impressive athlete who could benefit from a change of scenery. Vance Joseph has gotten the most out of lots of players, and Henley has the tools to be next in line."

Tesla and Cars &amp; Coffee Organizers Sued After Alleged 83-MPH Test Drive Ends in Head-On Crash

📅 Nov 12, 2026  |  Category: Business  |  Read Source →

A California woman claims a Tesla representative was showing off a Model Y's performance for photographers when he crossed into oncoming traffic and struck her SUV.

A 70-year-old California woman who reportedly suffered serious injuries in a head-on collision with a Tesla Model Y in the Bay Area this summer has filed a lawsuit against the automaker, the driver, and the organizers of a nearby Cars & Coffee event.

According to a civil complaint filed in San Mateo County Superior Court, a Tesla representative was demonstrating the vehicle's performance for event photographers. The driver allegedly reached speeds exceeding 80 miles per hour on a stretch of road with posted speed limits of 35–40 mph; the complaint claims he lost control on a blind curve, and crashed into Michelle Brooks's Honda CR-V.

The incident occurred on August 30, 2026, according to the filing, along State Route 84 near La Honda, California. The lawsuit identifies the Tesla driver as 21-year-old Micah Zuglian, who was allegedly driving a company-owned Model Y while attending a Cars & Coffee gathering at Ambassador Wine Garden. According to the complaint, Zuglian took two photographers associated with the event for a drive to demonstrate the Tesla's performance. Images allegedly captured during the drive show the vehicle's speedometer displaying 83 mph; another image reportedly shows a speed of 61 mph near a posted 35-mph speed limit sign.

The lawsuit alleges that Zuglian subsequently crossed the double-yellow lines on the road and struck Brooks's 2014 Honda CR-V head-on. Brooks had to be transported to Stanford Hospital for treatment of her injuries, which reportedly included fractured vertebrae, fractured ribs, broken limbs, and a traumatic brain injury.

The complaint alleges that representatives from the California Highway Patrol, San Mateo County Sheriff's Office, and a county supervisor's office met with event organizers beforehand and urged them to cancel the gathering over traffic safety concerns. The suit claims that roads in the area are notorious for racing, and that in the past five years, more than 20 people have died and almost 800 have been injured in automobile crashes in the vicinity.

Brooks alleges that Tesla negligently entrusted the Model Y to its representative and failed to adequately supervise him. The complaint also alleges negligence by the event organizers for proceeding despite the reported safety concerns. The plaintiff is seeking damages and requesting a jury trial.

The lawsuit also names Ethan Garabedian, doing business as “9burgring,” which organized the gathering, and Ambassador Wine Bars LLC, which hosted it. The allegations have not been proven in court, and the filing of a lawsuit does not establish liability on the part of Tesla, Zuglian, or the event organizers.

Google Wallet can finally unlock doors with digital home keys

📅 Nov 12, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Posts from this topic will be added to your daily email digest and your homepage feed.

Posts from this topic will be added to your daily email digest and your homepage feed.

Posts from this topic will be added to your daily email digest and your homepage feed.

Android users can add digital keys for compatible smart locks in Google Home to their Wallet app.﻿

Android users can add digital keys for compatible smart locks in Google Home to their Wallet app.﻿

Posts from this author will be added to your daily email digest and your homepage feed.

Posts from this author will be added to your daily email digest and your homepage feed.

Google has added digital home key support to Google Wallet, allowing Android users to open smart locks by tapping their phone against them or simply approaching. The feature is currently available in public preview for the Google Home app, and will roll out to everyone “over the coming weeks,” according to Google’s announcement.

This was a long time coming, given Samsung and Apple users have had this convenience for a while now. While Google Home already has some unlocking functionality, this update allows users to leverage the Near Field Communication (NFC) and Ultra-Wideband (UWB) tech in their phones with compatible smart locks. Multiple locks can be controlled via Google Wallet using a single digital home key. All household members linked to the same Google Home account can also add their own key to their personal Google Wallet.

Tap to Unlock works by holding the back of an NFC-enabled Android phone near the lock reader, and doesn’t require the phone to be unlocked. Unlock on Approach needs to be manually enabled in Google Wallet, but can automatically unlock a door when it detects the user walking up.

There are a few compatibility requirements to bear in mind, however. These new Google Home capabilities are built on Aliro, the open standard designed to let any smartphone unlock any smart lock, but the hardware that supports it is still fairly limited. According to Google’s announcement, the list of supported locks currently includes the Aqara u400, Schlage Sense Pro (NFC at launch and UWB to follow), and Nuki Keypad 2 NFC, with “more locks to come.”

Users will also need a Matter-enabled Google Hub with Thread Border Router, such as the 2nd-gen Nest Hub. Tap to unlock requires NFC-enabled phones running Android 9 or later, and hands-free unlocking only works on Aliro UWB-compatible Android phones, such as the Google Pixel 10 Pro series and above. Wear OS smartwatches aren’t supported “at launch,” according to Google, which implies they might be in the future, and that seems likely given that Apple Watch already has similar unlocking features.

Probably Stolen Lou quest walkthrough: How to complete and all rewards

📅 Nov 12, 2026  |  Category: Business  |  Read Source →

The game’s most wanted criminal, no NPC in Probably Stolen can rival or challenge the mysterious (& dangerous) Lou.

And as you can expect, her cryptic persona persists in her quest, which can only be triggered and completed by playing a dangerous game. Here’s a full walkthrough on how to complete the Lou storyline in Probably Stolen.

Unlike most NPCs, like Serpico and Eleanor, Lou only appears on a random day after you purchase the Business Permit, available in the fifth week. During her visit, she will introduce herself before asking you for a series of ”tools” to be used on her next job. To trigger her quest and start her storyline, call Security on her and claim the 1.500 Credits bounty.

Now that you surprised Lou and made the questionable choice of turning in a specialist, a Republic Operative will pay you a visit in the next week, claiming Lou to be a traitor to the Republic and an alleged terrorist. He also gives you a Republic Transmitter and makes an offer: plant it into something and give it to Lou for 700 Credits, as she is sure to visit you again.

Sure enough, Lou will surprise you in a few weeks, inviting you to play a game and letting you know what’s at stake. The next morning, she reveals that a bomb is hidden within your wares and gives you until midnight to disarm it. Upon review, the Plastic Explosive will prove to be fake.

Read what’s behind the latest news in gaming (with a bit of snark) by subscribing to the Destructoid Checkpoint newsletter!

Following your fake brush with death, Lou will pay a visit to the store in around five days and leave her ”Box of Toys” in your care, before warning you not to touch it. The box features a document revealing more about her past and ties with the Republic.

She returns to grab it the next day. If you haven’t moved the contents of the box (you can still read the document at no risk) or placed the transmitter in it, she allows you to pick either 1x Advanced Turbo Booster, 1x Smuggler’s Bay, 1x Backpack (Small), or 2x Module Expansion Kits as a reward.

Following the encounter above, just keep playing until Lou visits under the guise of an inspector. She reveals that her ”guest’ has finally given her the info she needs, before asking for 750 Credits (or 500 depending on your chosen difficulty) worth of weapons, armor, or meds as a trade for the bounty you received earlier. You have one week to secure it. If you can’t, she asks for the amount in Credits on the due date.

We are now at the final stretch of her storyline, as Lou will thank you and vanish upon getting the agreed-upon ”tools” (or the necessary Credits) in her next visit. This will be your final meeting, with the news of the assassination of an admiral stamping the papers the next morning. She will send you a note, though, together with a Storage Bay Expansion Kit.

Lou’s quest/storyline then ends with another visit from the Republic Operative, who reveals they plan to pursue her and hints at your involvement in the whole ordeal.

With Lou’s quest done, don’t forget to also check out how to produce Huge Nutrifruits and unlock the Green Thumb achievement.
