How Kelly Rowland Keeps Her Eczema Flare-Ups Under Control During Winter

📅 Nov 12, 2026  |  Category: Health  |  Read Source →

Follow Healthline on our socials for all things health.

Wintertime can bring everyone down with its colder weather and less sunlight. But for those with eczema, this time of year can wreak havoc on their skin, too.

That’s the case for Grammy-winning singer, actress, and Destiny’s Child alum Kelly Rowland, who has been living with eczema since her 20s. During the winter months, she often dreads the cold.

“[You] don’t know what the environment is going to give you—because mine is also environmental—or what the weather is going to give you,” Rowland told Healthline.

Cold weather and indoor heating can reduce humidity, drying out the skin and triggering or worsening eczema flare-ups.

“[What we know] about the skin barrier is that it really is like a gate or wall against the outside world,” Camille Howard-Verovic, MD, a dermatologist at Active Dermatology, told Healthline.

In healthy skin, the barrier controls what enters and leaves the skin. However, for people with eczema, Howard-Verovic said they have skin barrier dysfunction, which allows substances such as allergens and irritants to penetrate the skin.

Cold weather can increase transepidermal water loss, a process in which moisture escapes through the skin’s outer barrier and evaporates into the surrounding air.

“People with eczema have a tremendous amount of transepidermal water loss, so you’re just drier,” said Howard-Verovic. “[You’re] not holding onto the water as well, so that causes the itching and the rash that we feel when we have eczema.”

Rowland first noticed eczema flares behind her ears as well as on her elbows, arms, and the back of her knees.

“[It] looked like a rash, so that’s what I thought it was all this time, and I dealt with it for so long until after I had my son and then it started to get worse on my face,” she said.

She eventually went to a dermatologist who diagnosed her with moderate-to-severe eczema, a chronic inflammatory skin condition that can cause intense itching, painful skin cracking, thickened skin, and redness or swelling.

When Rowland learned that eczema can be genetic, it made sense to her.

“I remember seeing big rashes on [my mom’s] neck and her arms…so she had it, and I guess I was the lucky one,” said Rowland.

At first, she tried eliminating foods that she thought might be triggers. She also bathed in oatmeal.

“[I] literally have had a tub full of oatmeal, like literally went to the store to get store-bought fresh oats,” she said. “I tried everything, and it doesn’t help until you go talk to a doctor about it.”

At first, she tried prescription topical medications but found them cumbersome.

“I don’t have the time, if I’m being honest, and we live in a world that is moving so fast…I started buying like three tubes of whatever it was I was using and losing it; it was just too much for me,” Rowland said.

Rowland said she found relief with Ebglyss, a prescription injectable medication for moderate-to-severe atopic dermatitis. She has also partnered with Eli Lilly and Company, its manufacturer, to raise awareness about eczema.

Rowland said she’s sharing her experience to help others living with eczema, including people she’s met who have experienced greater challenges managing the condition.

“One lady was telling me about how she’s having a tough time [looking] in the mirror cause she doesn’t know who’s looking back at her, and she saw her skin before eczema and after,” said Rowland.

When she first had symptoms, she said she felt embarrassed, too.

“[Because] it was just in the middle of my arm space and…[if I] put out my hand to shake someone’s hand, you don’t know if somebody’s judgy,” she said.

Rowland hopes being open about her condition will encourage others to advocate for themselves and talk with their doctor about treatment options.

Because eczema can look different on deeper skin tones, Howard-Verovic noted that a dermatologist can help make the correct diagnosis.

“Some people may not even know that they have eczema,” she said. “We know that eczema is a rash; it can appear red on certain skin tones, and it can be grey or white on certain skin tones, so it can look a little different.”

In addition to medication, Rowland said managing stress helps reduce her eczema flare-ups.

“Before [when] I went on tour, because I was under so much stress, it flared up like crazy, like you wouldn’t believe,” she said. “We were in the back trying to figure out how to cover certain things up, and we figured some of it out, and some of it was really tricky, but what helped was when I actually just calmed down…not that it helped overnight, but it did just kind of bring the flare down.”

She said red light therapy, feeling grounded outside without her shoes on, getting vitamin D from natural sunlight, and cooking are ways she reduces ongoing stress.

“Cooking calms me when my kids aren’t here, and I have a really good [Zen] playlist on,” said Rowland. “[If] you ask me a question [in those moments], I might give you my heart; I’m just so happy.”

She also tries to cut back on sugar, which she believes triggers flares.

Another way to reduce flares, according to Howard-Verovic, is to avoid hot showers, especially in the winter.

“[We] know eczematous skin likes lukewarm water,” she said.

Because Howard-Verovic and her daughter both live with eczema, she set a rule about when it’s time to get out of the shower.

“[If] you see condensation on the bathroom mirror, you’ve spent too much time in the shower,” said Howard-Verovic. “[That] really hot, hot water feels really good in that moment…but when you get out all that water evaporates, and there’s no moisture in the atmosphere, and it dries your skin out, and now your skin is unhappy again.”

If you prefer to take baths, she said use gentle cleansers.

“[There] are bleach baths for really, really bad flare-ups,” she noted.

Diluted bleach baths are sometimes recommended for people with eczema, but they should only be used according to a healthcare professional’s instructions.

As soon as you get out of the shower or bath, Howard-Verovic said to apply a quality moisturizer to your damp skin. According to the National Eczema Association (NEA), lotions containing the following ingredients can help ease dry skin associated with eczema.

To help identify appropriate products, look for ones that have the NEA’s Seal of Acceptance.

Google’s Pixel Tag tracker won’t work as well on older Android phones – check our list

📅 Nov 12, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Our gifting experts are obsessively searching for holiday gifts that are unique, thoughtful and suitable for any budget.

Tech > Services & Software > Operating Systems > Mobile OS > Android

Several of the tracker’s best features require Bluetooth 6.0-compatible device.

Google’s Pixel Tag, the company’s take on an Android-exclusive Bluetooth tracker, is finally here, following its August announcement during the Google I/O developer conference.

Also: Google’s new Android XR glasses have lower ‘creep factor’ than competitors

Pixel Tag is a complete Bluetooth tracker for Android users that appears less confined to device-specific ecosystems than Samsung’s Smart Tag 3 and Apple’s AirTag.

However, if you look at Google’s footnotes, the Pixel Tag’s functionality is diminished when an older Android phone is involved. Several of the tracker’s best features require a compatible device running Android 9.0+ and Bluetooth 6.0+, according to Google.

Android 9 and newer encompasses a lot of devices, but Bluetooth 6.0 and newer doesn’t. Here’s what to check before you buy.

Smartphones began shipping with Bluetooth 6.0 in 2025, starting with the Samsung Galaxy S26 Plus and Galaxy S26 Ultra. The following phones also have Bluetooth 6.0:

If your Android phone is older than any of the listed above, even by a generation, it likely supports Bluetooth 5.3 or 5.4 — not enough for the Pixel Tag’s best features.

The Pixel Tag’s precision-finding capabilities are powered by Bluetooth Channel Sounding, a key feature introduced in Bluetooth 6.0. Nearly every review I’ve seen of the Pixel Tag, including our own, tests the tracker when paired with a Pixel 10, Pixel 10 Pro, Pixel 11, or Pixel 11 Pro.

I assume that Android users whose phones run Android 9.0+ but lack Bluetooth 6.0+ can use the Pixel Tag for very basic, general location tracking. These users may see the tracker’s general location, but Google’s device compatibility footnote appears several times throughout its press release.

The following features require Android 9.0+ and Bluetooth 6.0+, according to Google’s release:

So, on older Android devices without Android 9.0+ and Bluetooth 6.0+, the following aren’t supported: locating the exact direction and distance of your tracked devices, pinging a tracker when you don’t see it, or giving your tracked devices a custom name.

Also: 3 ways Bluetooth 7 upgrades your devices beyond lossless audio

Additionally, shorter-distance tracking requires an ultra-wideband chip inside your Android phone. Channel Sounding handles distance and direction at longer ranges, while UWB takes over for shorter ranges. Most premium Android phones with Bluetooth 6.0 also have UWB chips, but several of Google’s base-model phones lack a UWB chip.

Samsung’s base-model, A-series, and Z Flip-series phones also lack UWB, resulting in more phones that can’t access the Pixel Tag’s short-range precision finding capabilities.

With all the specs in mind, the Pixel Tag is the best option for users of recent premium flagship Android phones. Those without the latest Bluetooth connectivity will find that the Pixel Tag works like any other Bluetooth tracker, with basic functionality.

Also: These are the closest things to AirTags for Android users

The good thing is that a single Pixel Tag is $30, so it’s not much more expensive than a Tile Tag, and cheaper than a Chipolo Loop. Still, if your phone doesn’t support Bluetooth 6.0, you might be better off looking for a cheaper option until you’re ready to upgrade.

Better 2.0 plan hits reported $34.1M in cost savings

📅 Nov 12, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Better reported $34.1M in annualized cost savings under its Better 2.0 turnaround, up $3.7M this week and moving closer to a $64.2M goal. The company posted a $36.5M Q3 net loss on $1.4B in originations and expanded its wholesale network to 43 brokerages.  AI Summary

Better Home & Finance Holding Co. announced on Tuesday that it has reached $34.1 million in annualized cost savings as it continues a turnaround under its Better 2.0 plan — which includes eliminating management and loan support positions while replacing some duties with artificial intelligence.

The latest figure, reported in the company’s fifth weekly scorecard, represents progress toward a $64.2 million cost-reduction target. Better also added five brokerages to its wholesale platform, advanced financing negotiations tied to the planned sale of its U.K. bank subsidiary and hired executive search firm Daversa Partners to lead its CEO search.

The additional $3.7 million in annualized savings reported this week comes from further reductions in mortgage operations and the corporate organization. Better previously reported $30.4 million in savings during the first two weeks of its turnaround initiative.

The latest scorecard, which is released each Tuesday until the company reaches adjusted EBITDA breakeven, follows Better’s third-quarter earnings reported on Monday. Founder Vishal Garg detailed additional workforce reductions, including the elimination of management layers and loan support roles that he said would be permanently replaced by AI tools.

During the quarter, the company recorded a net loss of $36.5 million, compared with a $30.6 million loss in the second quarter and a $39.1 million loss in the same period last year. The company originated $1.4 billion in loans during the quarter, up 15% year over year.

Better has removed managers representing more than $3.5 million in annualized savings, Garg told analysts. The company also eliminated its home lending associate position, which handled early customer outreach and application preparation before transferring borrowers to loan officers. Garg confirmed that these responsibilities are now handled by Better’s AI system, Betsy.

In the latest scorecard, Better said its wholesale platform now includes 43 brokerages, representing 1,382 brokers and $378 million in trailing 12-month origination volume. That compares with 39 signed brokerages reported during the company’s earnings call.

Better also launched its AI-powered home equity line of credit (HELOC) product with a large existing partner. The company plans to complete live customer testing before ramping up to full partnership volume. It is also working with three other major partners and pursuing a revived large-scale partnership.

On the capital markets front, Better received five term sheets for approximately $50 million in debt financing tied to its planned sale of Birmingham Bank. The company is negotiating terms with prospective financing partners.

As for Better’s CEO search with Daversa Partners, the next steps outlined in the scorecard include finalizing the candidate profile and beginning outreach.

You can keep up with strike analytics, scores, fight cards and other details.

UFC fans are getting another way to track matches, stats and fighters on their Apple devices. On Thursday, Apple announced the addition of the sports organization to the Apple Sports app, which joins a lineup that already includes NHL, NFL, NBA, MLB, college sports, golf and other leagues. This launch comes just ahead of UFC 334 on Saturday, where the main event is Ciryl Gane versus Josh Hokit.

Fans can follow fight night match cards that show every bout, from preliminaries to the main event. Whoop’s New ‘Verifiably Human’ Social Network Feature Lets You Share Health Data

📅 Nov 12, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Our gifting experts are obsessively searching for holiday gifts that are unique, thoughtful and suitable for any budget.

Our expert, award-winning staff selects the products we cover and rigorously researches and tests our top picks. If you buy through our links, we may earn a commission.

Humans allows members to follow, share, comment and react to each other’s data and achievements.

Whoop, maker of the Whoop 5.0 fitness tracker, launched a new app feature that lets you follow and share data with each other, so they can support friends and family with reactions and comments. Whoop Founder and CEO Will Ahmed called the feature, dubbed Humans, the “most verifiably human network ever launched” in a press release, as each account represents a real person with a verified heartbeat.

Through Humans, Whoop members can share their achievements and the sleep, strain, activity and recovery data collected by their wearable. According to the release, this makes Humans different from your average social media network, which is filled with curated and AI-generated content, because users can’t fake their physiological data.

So it’s not just about the numbers, Whoop is also launching Moments, a feature that will let members share workout photos and personal experiences.

According to Whoop, members control what they share, who can see their content and who follows them. These settings can be updated at any time.

Whoop doesn’t monetize or sell members’ personal health data.

Activities that members designate as sensitive won’t be shared by default. There’s also “stealth mode,” which lets members temporarily hide their information.

Whoop already has a feature called Teams, which allows you to join groups and compete on leaderboards. The release stated that 57% of members belong to at least one Whoop Team.

Even with the addition of Humans, Whoop is keeping Teams. The only change will be its placement under the app’s Community tab. According to a Whoop spokesperson, the Teams feature will now appear below the Humans feature under that tab.

Whoop Humans is now available in the Whoop app. To participate, you’ll need a Whoop wearable, which is included in memberships ranging from $199 to $359 a year.

World’s first ‘dark magic mushroom’ discovered in Florida

📅 Nov 12, 2026  |  Category: Science  |  Read Source →

Published Nov 12, 2026 11:59 AM EST

A mushroom recently discovered in Florida looks unassuming, but this fungus

packs a serious and surprising punch. According to mycologists writing in the journal Current Biology, Galerina indica is the first-known “dark magic mushroom,” a species featuring hefty doses of both psychedelic and poisonous compounds.

Depending on their chemical makeup, many mushrooms will do one of two things. Fungi packed with psilocybin can send users on an extensive psychedelic trip, while varieties laden with amatoxins can be poisonous. Although mycologists have long assumed a single species could hypothetically feature both traits, no one had ever documented such an example. A recent field survey changed that, however.

“I could hardly believe it. We knew it was possible, but tried not to get too excited until we did a more rigorous analysis,” Andrew Kunik, a former University of Florida plant pathologist and study co-author, said in a statement.

Evolutionary biologists suspect certain mushrooms developed their poisonous amatoxins as a protection against foraging animals, although hallucinogenic psilocybin’s emergence and purpose in fungi remains unclear. Regardless, both compounds found their way into G. indica. University of Utah mycologist and study co-author Bryn Dentinger believes that a process called horizontal gene transfer is likely to blame—although it’s a completely novel discovery.

“Horizontal gene transfer is like if we shook hands and some of my DNA incorporated into your genome,” he said. “[But] two independent horizontal gene transfer events for these specialized metabolites in one mushroom genus are unheard of.”

For as unique as G. indica is, it is very easy to miss. The small, innocuous brown mushrooms are barely the size of a thumbnail, and resemble countless other fungi.

“These mushrooms are really hard to find,” added study co-author, University of Florida plant pathologist, and state mycologist Matthew Smith. “It’s hard to differentiate them from others, even for a trained expert.”

Although difficult, documenting the specimens is not only vital to the field of mycology. It can help save lives.

“Part of my job as Florida’s state mycologist is to identify poisonous or potentially poisonous species,” explained Smith. “So it is important to know what species we have and whether they might cause problems for people or pets.”

Existing home sales hit a new low as rates stay high

📅 Nov 12, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Existing home sales slowed to a 3.75 million annual pace in October, down 5.5% MoM and 8.8% YoY, with declines across all regions. Inventory increased to 1.59 million and the median price rose 0.4% YoY to $416,700.  AI Summary

The annual pace of existing home sales continues to decline compared to a year ago. In October, existing home sales slowed to an annual pace of 3.75 million, according to data published on Thursday by the National Association of Realtors (NAR).

This represents a 5.5% decline from a month ago and an 8.8% drop from October 2025.

“Home buying is difficult when mortgage rates have climbed to their highest levels since 2000, aside from a single month in 2023. As a result, home sales fell to their lowest level since August 2010,” Lawrence Yun,” NAR’s chief economist, said in a statement.

HousingWire Data shows similar trends, noting that for the week ending November 6, 2026, an estimated 74,473 single family homes were sold, down 4.5% compared to a year ago. In addition, the median sales price declined 3.2% to $400,000, while active inventory rose 7.2% from a year ago to 842,242 single family homes.

Regionally, existing home sales fell in all-four regions compared to a year prior. The Northeast, which reported an annual sales rate of 470,000 existing homes, recorded the smallest decline at 4.1%, while the West (650,000 existing homes) reported the largest at 14.5%. On a month-over-month basis, the sales pace remained unchanged in the Northeast, but fell in the West (-9.7%), South (-6.5%) and the Midwest (-3.2%). The South reported an annual sales pace of 1.73 million homes in October, while the Midwest recorded a pace of 900,000 existing homes.

At the end of the month, there were 1.59 million existing homes for sale, up 1.3% from a month prior and 4.6% from a year ago. This inventory represents 5.1 months’ supply at the current sales pace.

Despite the slower market conditions, the median sales price continued to rise, jumping 0.4% year-over-year in October to $416,700. This marks the 40th consecutive month of year-over-year price increases, according to NAR.

Reflecting the slower pace of home sales, the median time on market for properties rose to 35 days, up from 33 days a month ago and 34 days a year ago, according to NAR’s Realtors Confidence Index.

NAR also reported that just 29% of sales were first-time buyers, down from 31% a month ago and 32% a year ago. Additionally, as mortgage rates remained at an elevated level in October, the share of all cash sales rose to 29%, compared to 27% last month. However, this is on par with a year ago.

NAR’s housing affordability index also fell, dropping from 107.3 a year ago to 100.7 in October 2026, with the index posting annual declines in all four major regions. The Northeast reported the largest annual decline of 8.7% and the West recorded the smallest at 5.2%.

“Housing affordability remains a major constraint, particularly when mortgage rates rise above the 7% psychological threshold for potential buyers,” Selma Hepp, the chief economist at Cotality and a 2025 HousingWire Vanguard award winner, said in a statement. “High rates are keeping monthly payments elevated, while tariff-related construction costs are adding pressure to insurance premiums and property taxes are further straining homeowners. Rising everyday expenses, including utility bills, are also squeezing household budgets. Together, these pressures are limiting momentum in existing-home sales.

Despite the worsening affordability challenges, just 2% of sales were distressed sales, unchanged from last month and a year ago.

Looking ahead, economists believe housing market activity will pick up if mortgage rates decline a meaningful amount.

“For housing, the key question is not simply whether mortgage rates decline, but whether they fall enough to meaningfully improve purchasing power and bring more buyers and sellers back to the market,” Hepp said. “Until then, I expect activity to remain relatively subdued, making the spring homebuying season an increasingly important test of whether the market can finally turn a corner.”

But for the next few months, industry leaders anticipate a slower pace of home sales, thanks to the level mortgage rates reached in mid-October.

“We could see further weakness, since buyers who stepped back when rates hit 7.4% in mid-October won’t show up as missing sales until November and December closings,” Sean Fergus, the vice president of economic research at NewHomeSource and Zonda, said in a statement. “Rates have since eased, but buyers need to trust that relief will last before they come back in force.”

Scout Motors has 212,000 vehicle reservations as $2 billion South Carolina plant nears completion

📅 2026-11-12  |  Category: Business  |  Read Source →

BLYTHEWOOD, S.C. — Electric vehicle startup Scout Motors has notched roughly 212,000 reservations for its upcoming EVs as the company nears completion of its new $2 billion plant in South Carolina, CEO Scott Keogh told CNBC.

The reservations are for the Volkswagen-backed company's Terra pickup truck and Traveler SUV, which are expected to start full production next year before deliveries begin to customers in 2028.

Reservations do not necessarily translate to sales, as the U.S. automotive industry has learned during the past decade, especially with highly anticipated all-electric trucks such as the Ford F-150 Lightning, Tesla Cybertruck and others that garnered strong interest that didn't lead to meaningful numbers of purchases.

Keogh said he expects Scout could roughly double those reservations by the time customer production begins, but he said the more important thing is to convert early interest into actual sales as production nears.

"It's been a consistent drumbeat," Keogh said during an interview at Scout's plant site outside Columbia, South Carolina. "I think we could easily be up over 400,000 with reservations without doing too much effort. The big thing for us is how much of the initial production can we 100% presell."

Keogh said achieving an order book of 12-to-18 months "would be great" and something he thinks the company can do before production. Scout on Thursday said construction is "in its final phase."

Scout's vehicles will be offered as all-electric versions as well as electric hybrid variants, known as extended-range electric vehicles, or EREVs, that include an engine as a backup generator for the electric motors and batteries.

Scout, a former American vehicle brand from 1961 to 1980, was expected to exclusively offer EVs in a bid for the German automaker to expand its presence in the U.S. However, slower-than-expected adoption of EVs and higher costs led the company to decide to offer EREVs as well.

Keogh said the EREVs and SUV are dominating reservations thus far, with roughly 87% choosing EREV models and 75% being SUVs.

The CEO also said the company is drawing interest from its target markets, which include owners of off-road vehicles such as the Ford Bronco and Jeep Wrangler as well as full-size pickup trucks and U.S. EV leader Tesla

Given the interest signaled by reservations, Keogh said the company is expected to launch with the EREVs first, followed by the EVs. He also said the company plans to launch with mid-level models first, a deviation from many companies that begin with their highest-priced models.

Both the Traveler and Terra are expected to start under $60,000. The EREV models are expected to feature more than 500 miles of range, according to the company, compared with 300 miles of range for the all-electric models.

"The No.1 thing for me is I want to go get the best volume as soon as possible," Keogh said. "In my mind, it's how quick can you get to 100,000 units? It's at 100,000 units where you can start to punch through."

His comments come months after Rivian Automotive received criticism online for launching its R2 SUV with its highest-priced models first, despite that being a common practice across the automotive industry.

Keogh said Scout's South Carolina plant is about 85% complete, but supporting infrastructure and a $300 million supplier park continue to be installed.

Reservations for Scout's vehicles initially opened in October 2024, when it revealed the Traveler and Terra, which feature sleek, yet rugged, exterior appearances that pay some homage to the brand's historic models.

<a href="https://www.fiercehealthcare.com/regulatory/onc-cms-launch-electronic-prior-authorization-testing-initiative" hreflang="en">ONC, CMS move electronic prior authorization into real-world testing</a>

📅 2026-11-12  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →
ONC, CMS move electronic prior authorization into real-world testing" style="max-width:100%; height:auto; border-radius:8px;" loading="lazy">

The Office of the National Coordinator for Health IT (ONC) is launching a voluntary industry testing initiative to help healthcare organizations prepare for federal electronic prior authorization requirements taking effect in 2027.

ONC is collaborating with the Centers for Medicare and Medicaid Services (CMS) to launch a series of industry testing events in production-like environments to advance end-to-end electronic prior authorization (ePA), the agencies announced Thursday.

A federal rule requires some payers to implement an application programming interface (API) to support electronic prior authorization processes by January 1. Impacted payers include Medicare Advantage, Medicaid managed care, CHIP and ACA exchange plans.

The focused, 10- to 12-week testing sprints will bring together payers, providers, hospitals, electronic health record (EHR) developers, intermediaries and other industry partners to test their own workflows across connected systems and identify gaps before broader implementation, CMS and ONC officials said.

"Federal regulations and industry standards have laid important groundwork for ePA, but our work is not done," Tom Keane, M.D., National Coordinator for Health IT, said in a statement. "With our colleagues at CMS, we are bringing partners together to advance the next phase of implementation testing to accelerate ePA progress in the near term."

Organizations interested in participating can learn more and sign up for the initiative by November 20, according to ONC. Participant feedback and lessons learned will inform future testing and implementation activities.

"Regulations and industry standards have laid important groundwork for ePA but real work remains to put this policy into practice. These events mark the next phase of implementation testing, bringing trading partners together to examine end-to-end workflows in production-like environments," ONC leaders Ashley Hain and Molly Prieto, principal deputy director of the Office of Standards, Certification and Analysis, wrote in a blog post.

During the testing sprints, participants will use their own workflows to examine how prior authorization requests and decisions move across connected systems. "The focus is on finding technical barriers and workflow gaps that could arise in real-world implementation, while organizations still have an opportunity to address them before broader deployment," Hain and Prieto wrote.

ONC and CMS will use the findings from the testing events to make practical improvements. "Some findings may point to standards that need clarification or revision. Others may reveal a need for clearer educational materials for developers, providers and hospitals, or more useful implementer guidance," Hain and Prieto wrote.

"We aim to identify critical updates quickly and help industry prioritize the improvements needed for successful implementation across care settings. The purpose is to turn the progress already made through regulations and standards into workflows that reduce administrative burden and support timely care," the ONC leaders wrote.

The testing events will include workflows and real-world scenarios using the HL7 FHIR Da Vinci implementation guides for Coverage Requirements Discovery (CRD), Documentation Templates and Rules (DTR), and Prior Authorization Support (PAS). Each event will include coordinated scenarios, based off high-volume clinical services that require prior authorization and supporting resources to help participants test together, ONC said.

ONC and CMS are working with the MITRE-led Health Federally Funded Research and Development Center and Drummond Group to support stakeholder recruitment, readiness discussions, participant matching and testing coordination. Drummond Group will operate FHIRplace, the virtual environment where developers, providers, payers and intermediaries will test with their trading partners.

CMS and ONC encouraged participation from organizations of all sizes, including smaller organizations and those serving rural communities.
